# Rules → formal rules

Every ownership rule of [`docs/MEMORY.md`](../docs/MEMORY.md) and the
phase that formalizes it. A rule is **done** when its typing rule is
stated, used in the soundness proof, and every hole probe it is meant to
reject is mapped to it by its `// map:` line (the table `corpus/map.tsv`,
written from those lines by [`tools/fuzz/holes/map.sh`](../tools/fuzz/holes/map.sh)).

Formal rules are Lean names in [`A1/`](A1/) and [`A2/`](A2/) (the same
names, extended): typing rules are constructors of `StmtT`, `RhsT`,
`PushArg` and the call check `CallT`; `kill`, `exitScope` and `derive`
are the context operations they use. A1's calculus has owned `Vec` trees
(a `String` is a `Vec` of bytes); A2 adds structs, enums, closures and
trait objects, panics and the drop journal; threads are A3.

| MEMORY.md | Rule | Phase | Formal rule | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §1, §7.6 | Single owner, drop flags, auto-drop | A1 | an owner's slot is `⟨own, some tree⟩`, a released or moved one `⟨own, none⟩` (the drop flag); `HeapOK` (one owner per allocation); `Step.scopeSkip` + `DropSlot`; `exitScope`; an early exit (`break`, `continue`, `^`) ends every scope it leaves as the scope's end does (`ExitAt`, `Step.blockExit`; typed by `Exits.under`, `Exits.underD`; Agora #871) | done |
| §2.1 | Moves — use after move | A1 / A2 | `RhsT.moveOwn`, `PushArg.move`, `CallT` (`sink`): the moved binding is dead (`live := false`) and every read needs a live entry; a moved argument is passed nowhere else in the call and reached from no other argument. A take out of a place that may be replaced (`RhsT.take`, `EvalR.take`, Agora #826: an owner or an `inout .replace` parameter, of a `Vec` whose elements hold no views): the `Vec` moves out, the place is killed as a replace kills it and keeps a fresh empty `Vec`, live and holding nothing — nurlc's hand-over of an `inout` handle parameter (Agora #776) | done |
| §2.1b | Releasing what the compiler releases | A1 / B | `StmtT.free` releases only a live owner and kills it; inside `unsafe` is B | done for safe code |
| §2.2 | Second names | A1 | `RhsT.moveOwn` (an owner moves), `RhsT.copyBor`, `RhsT.copyRoot`, `RhsT.borrow` (a borrow is copied and depends on the root, `derive`) | done |
| §2.3 | Stack references must not escape | A1 / A2 | for borrows and views: `exitScope` ends them with their root, `FnOK` lets a result depend only on parameters; a closure's captures by handle are what it depends on (`RhsT.pack`: `D`), by name what it holds (`capDeps`), ended with their binding (`exitScope`, `kill`) | done |
| §2.4 | Exclusive access for `inout` arguments | A1 / A2 | `CallT`: an argument that moves or may change is not lent out (`F`), is passed once, and is reached from no other argument; `paramOK`, `canDo` (the `inout` bound); an `inout` argument of any owning type is its owner, never a borrow or a copy (`paramOK_inout`, h295), and a summary pair on one names a kind inside it (`kindFits`) | done |
| §2.5 | Iterator invalidation | A1 | a borrow of `xs` depends on `xs`: `StmtT.push` (`kill .realloc`), `RhsT.pop` (`kill .drop`), `StmtT.assign` and `StmtT.free` (`kill .replace`) end it | done |
| §2.6 | Loop-carried moves | A1 / A2 | `StmtT.while`, `StmtT.loop` (`while T`): the body ends in the context it starts in; a `break` or `continue` is an `exit` of the block around the loop or its body, whose end says no more than the context each exit leaves from (`StmtT.block`, `Exits.blk`; Agora #871) | done |
| §2.7 | Escape through a callee | A1 / A2 / A3 | `CallT` against the callee's summary: what the result borrows (`lends`) and holds (`rholds`), what the callee stores into `inout` arguments (`outHolds`), its effect bound on each; `FnOK` checks the body against it; a closure handed to a callee is held by its result (`heldOfArgs`) and run as the callee may (`NamesFree`, `mutNames`); threads are A3 | done |
| §2.8 | Escape through a return | A1 / A2 | `FnOK` (the result depends only on what `lends` / `rholds` name; a `^` before the body's end is an `exit` of the body's block, so it is checked at that end, Agora #871), `callRes` at the call; a call through a package lends from the receiver too (`recvRes`) | done |
| §2.10 | Views end when their source may reallocate | A1 / A2 | `RhsT.view`, `RhsT.sat`; `StmtT.push` (`Eff.realloc` ends views, and element borrows when elements hold views); `StmtT.editB` (a push, a truncation or an element set through a borrow kills every memory root of its path: `.drop`, or `.realloc` for a push through a same-typed `.self` copy); `PushArg` / `held` (a view in a container is still a view); `outHolds` (a callee storing a view into an argument) | done through an owner, an `inout` binding, or any borrow of elements that hold no views (`StmtT.editB`, agora #295); what stays outside is in `CLAIM.md` |
| §2.12 | A stored value belongs to its owner | A1 / A2 | `PushArg.move`, `CallT` (`sink`); aggregate literals (`RhsT.pair`, `RhsT.inl`, `RhsT.inr`) move or copy what they store, a field taken out (`takel`, `taker`, `unpair`) moves; a field replaced in place is the struct taken apart (`StmtT.unpair`, an owner or an `inout` struct it may replace) and assigned again (`StmtT.assign`), ending every borrow of the struct | done |
| §2.13 | A raw string in a value is a view | A1 / A2 | a string view is a `slice` entry (`RhsT.view`) depending on its owner; a view stored in an aggregate or a package makes that value depend on what it views (`ViewsOK`, `held`) | done |
| §2.11, §7.5 | Closures hold their captures | A2 | `RhsT.pack` (`CapsOK`: captures by handle are depended on, by name held, each read or changed within its bound); `StmtT.callc` (the call through a package: `CallT` on the code with the captures as `inout` arguments of their own, `NamesFree` — a binding a closure may change is changed by the call and reached from no argument but by a closure that changes it the same way — and no argument is such a binding); `sigCtx` / `symNames` (a closure a callee is handed may change the callee's `inout` parameters); `ValOK.pkg` / `envDeps` (the environment); `A2/CAPTURES_DESIGN.md` | done |
| §7.6 | A destructor (`% Drop`) runs at its binding's scope end, normal or panic path | A2 | `StmtT.letd` / `StmtT.scopeD` (a binding with a destructor, `DtorOK`: one `in` parameter, an integer result, lends and holds nothing); `Step.scopeDEnd` (the destructor's call, then `scope x` frees the tree), `Step.scopeDMoved` / `scopeDPMoved` (a moved binding runs none), `Step.scopeDP` (the unwind stops at the frame and leaves it at its end, `seq (scopeD x f skip) panic`: its end, `scopeDEnd`, runs the destructor, then the panic is raised again; Agora #790: what the unwinding orphaned is reaped inside the frame first); `unwindCtx`, `Inv.pinOut` / `PinOK`; a type that holds views: `letd` arms the binding (`Entry.arm`), `DtorSafe` / `DbindSafe` / `EndOK` keep its views alive, `MInv.armed` keeps them valid while its slot owns a value, `Entry.revive` hands them to the destructor | done, views included (A2-drop.1, A2-drop.2: agora #221, #241); on the type, not the binding: A2-drop.3 (agora #253) |
| §7.2, §7.3 | Panics and the drop journal | A2 | the panic steps unwind to `StmtT.catchR` (`recover`: `StmtT.letrec`, `recStmt`) through the journal, dropping what each scope owns (`A2/Panic.lean`); `no_leak_end` (`A2/Leak.lean`) | done |
| §7.7 | Reference-count cycles | B | `Rc` and its collector are the standard library's `unsafe` base | not started |
| §6.5 | Threads: `Send`, `Sync`, `Arc`, `Mutex`, `Locked`, `RwLocked` and `% Frozen`, `Atomic`, `PFile`, channels | A3 | A3.0, threads that only move: `SpawnOK` (a spawned function takes only `sink` parameters of types that hold no views), `TStep.spawn` (the arguments move to the new thread, the spawner's slots are left empty, through A2's `CallT`), `Touch` / `Race`, and the configuration invariant `CInv` (each thread A2's `ThreadOK` on its own part of the address space) — `A3.claim`. Then each shared kind as a block: `Arc` (A3.1: `RhsT.arcNew` / `arcGet`'s frozen lend, `ThruOK.noShare`, the sweep), `Locked` and `Mutex` (A3.2: `withLock` / `scopeL`, `Blocked`, `Rhs.lockTake` / `lockRestore`, `StmtT.lockedGet` / `lockedSet` / `lockedApply` / `lockedAwait` / `mutexLock`), `RwLocked` and `% Frozen` / `% NotSend` (A3.3: a read is a view block, `Stmt.withRead`, `Rhs.rwTryRead` / `rwTryWrite`, `Stmt.rwGet`, `NoGuard`, `Step.scopeLP`, `Step.waitPanic`), `Atomic` and channels (A3.4: `Rhs.atomic`, `Rhs.chanSend` / `chanRecv` / `chanClose`), library handles (A3.5: `Val.cell`, `SendCheck`, `TStep.spawnFail`, `CellInv`, `StmtT.withCellIn`, `Race := AddrRace ∨ CellRace`), closures that cross (A3.5b: `Ty.fn ms q`, `Cross`, `StmtT.callcE`), trait objects (A3.5c, by A3.5b's rules: a trait's bound is its objects' class, `TraitBound`, `TraitAt`, `StmtT.callc_bound`, `ImplOK`, and `SendCheck` walks the box), module globals (A3.6: `Cfg.gl`, `Rhs.glob`, `Ty.sync`, `GlobsOK`, `Made`, `A3.claim_made`) | done (`A3.claim`, `A3.claim_made`; `A3/DESIGN.md` and `CLAIM.md` list what stays outside). Probes: `race_shared_vec`, `thread_reads_freed`, `chan_send_then_use`, `slice_crosses_thread`, `thread_captures_owned_string`, `thread_closure_capture_used_after_spawn`, `closure_run_after_its_thread_copy_took_the_capture`, `spawned_closure_arm_payload` (A3.0); the `Arc`, lock, library-handle, closure, trait-object and global probes name their rules in `A3/DESIGN.md` |
| §3, §6.4 | `unsafe` boundary and the trusted base | B | — | not started |

## nurlc forms the calculus reads as forms it has (phase C)

Phase C proves that every program nurlc accepts is well-typed in the
formal rules. For a few of nurlc's forms that proof goes through a
reading of the form as one the calculus already has, not through a rule
of its own. These are the readings for a change to a Vec's or a String's
elements made through something other than its owner (Agora #295, step
0):

| nurlc form | Read as | Formal rule |
|---|---|---|
| A function that changes the elements of a Vec or String it takes as a plain parameter, as the standard library's mutators (`vec_push`, `vec_set`, `vec_clear`, `string_push_*`) take theirs | an `inout b` parameter, `b` the most it does: `realloc` if it only grows it, `drop` if it may drop elements, never `replace` (a plain parameter is not rebound) | `CallT`, `paramOK_inout`, `callPostAux` (the kill by the bound) |
| A change through a copy of one owner's whole handle (`: x ( id vs )`, `: ~ c vs`, a cursor), to its elements only | the same change made on that owner | `StmtT.push`, `RhsT.pop` |
| A change through a field path of an owner (`( vec_push [u] . o kids x )`) | the struct taken apart, its field changed, the struct put back (§2.12) | `StmtT.unpair`, `StmtT.push`, `StmtT.assign` |
| `vec_clear`, `vec_truncate`, `vec_insert`, `vec_remove` | loops of pops and pushes; `vec_remove v i` directly (`RhsT.remove`, A2-drop.3b; any index since Agora #607) | `RhsT.pop`, `RhsT.remove`, `StmtT.push` |

A reading holds only where nurlc's summary of the callee is at least what
the callee does: Agora #315 was a hole in that summary. A replacing
callee (`inout replace`) given a copy is no reading: the replacement
lands in the copy's own slot, so nurlc refuses it (h295, h367–h370). A
change through any other borrow (an element of a Vec of Vecs, a field
viewer, a join of borrows, a call's lent result) needs no reading:
`StmtT.editB` types it (Agora #295, step 5). What stays outside is in
`CLAIM.md`.

## Changes to MEMORY.md and what they do to the proof

Every change to the rules of `docs/MEMORY.md` is recorded here with its
effect on the formal rules: whether it touches a rule that is proven
(**done** above), and whether it **extends** the calculus (a new rule, or
a rule made to reject more: every existing proof still holds) or
**breaks** a proof (a rule made to accept more, which reopens its lemmas).

Each row lives in a file of its own, `formal/rules/t<N>.md` after its
change's Agora task (`t0.md`: the changes that had none), and the table
is written from those files in task order by `tools/formal_rules.py`
(Agora #299). A change adds or edits its own file and never the table,
so two changes never edit the same lines. The release regenerates the
table: until then, a change made since the last release is in its file
only.

<!-- BEGIN change rows: generated by tools/formal_rules.py from formal/rules/t*.md -->
| Change | MEMORY.md | Touches a proven rule? | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| A closure over a stack binding by pointer handed to any call that detaches it — `thread_spawn_owned`, `spawn`, `spawn_owned`, `spawn_joinable`, `signal_install`, as `thread_spawn` already was — is rejected: what runs it may outlive the frame (h503–h505) | §2.11 | no: a closure kept past its frame is the escape `CallT` already refuses for a keeping callee | extends: rejects more |
| A field of a call's result handed to a callee that takes it is a copy, unless the field left with the read (the call handed its result over, h266); one that cannot be copied is rejected (h424, h425) | §1, §7.6 | no: a lent value is a borrow, which is copied, not moved (`RhsT.copyBor`) | none to the proof: the compiler brought into line with A1 |
| A standard-library struct with its own `% Drop` (the state behind a handle) is sealed: a literal of it or a write to its fields is raw memory, as for a struct with a raw-pointer field (h444, h445); a library helper that needs its caller to size its operands or check its offsets is `raw` (h436–h438, h447–h470) | §3, §6.4 | no: the trusted base is B, not started | extension: safe code is rejected more; B takes the library's sealed structs and `raw` helpers as part of the base |
| A value that needs a drop — an option or a result of a String as well as a handle — kept by a callee is stored in the container it is handed with, so reading it after the container is released is a use after move (h421–h423) | §2.12 | no: a stored value belongs to its owner (`PushArg.move`, `held`) | extends: rejects more |
| A `~` loop's container (a binding, a field of one, or the struct a field it iterates belongs to) given a new value in the body, handed to a callee that takes it, or reached by a closure the body runs that assigns it or hands it to a changing or taking callee is rejected (h416, h418–h420) | §2.5 | no: a borrow of `xs` depends on `xs`, and an assignment or a release ends it (`StmtT.assign`, `StmtT.free`: `kill .replace` / `.drop`) | extends: rejects more |
| A field of an `inout` parameter handed to a callee that takes it is a replacement of that field in the function's summary (an `s` edge of `resolve_replaces`), so the caller's borrows of it end at the call (h417) | §2.4 | no: an `inout` argument that may change is not lent out (`CallT`, `F`) | extends: rejects more |
| A parameter whose value the function moves on through a cursor over it — an option parameter's payload handed back by a `??`, through a local, in a generic — is an inferred `sink` too, not only kept (h408–h415) | §1 | no: a payload taken out (`takel`, `taker`) moves the option, and a `sink` argument is dead after the call (`CallT` (`sink`)) | none to the proof: the compiler's summary brought into line with A1's `CallT` |
| A parameter whose field the function hands to a callee that takes it is an inferred `sink`: the function already owned it (kept) and dropped the rest, and now its caller's binding is moved by the call (h389–h393, h396, h397) | §1 | no: in the calculus a field taken out (`takel`, `taker`, `unpair`) moves its struct, and a `sink` argument is dead after the call (`CallT` (`sink`)) | none to the proof: the compiler's summary brought into line with A1's `CallT` |
| A field handed to a callee that takes it, from a binding whose copy alone the compiler would empty — a `vec_at` copy, a field viewer, a closure's capture — is rejected (`pendfx` / `pendfc` rows); one of a `vec_get` element, an option payload, a cursor or an `inout` parameter is emptied where it lives (h394, h395, h398–h400) | §7.6 | no: in the calculus only a live owner moves (`RhsT.moveOwn`); a borrow is copied (`RhsT.copyBor`) | extends: rejects more |
| A field handed to a parameter that takes it ends the views of its binding as a field replaced does, not as elements changed (h401–h404) | §2.11, §2.12 | no: a borrow or a capture by handle depends on its root (`derive`, `RhsT.pack`), and a field taken out moves the struct | extends: rejects more |
| A field a closure gives a new value through its by-reference capture releases the old value under the binding's drop flag (carried by the env); a binding that only borrows its value cannot be given one there (h405–h407) | §2.11 | no: a capture by name is held by the closure (`RhsT.pack`, `CapsOK`), and a field replaced in place is the struct taken apart and rebuilt (§2.12) | extends: rejects more |
| What a returned value that may hold a closure lends (`retclo`) is read off its source expression at the `^`, through the frame's closures, views and borrows (a bound closure, a join, a reassigned binding, a captured view), not only a literal capturing the parameter by name (h384–h388) | §2.11 | no: in the calculus a returned package's dependencies are its captures' (`RhsT.pack`, `FnOK`), whatever names them | none to the proof: the compiler's summary brought into line with A2's `FnOK` |
| A view of a call's temporary argument is used inside a call that consumes it; kept past it (bound, returned, assigned, stored, taken by a join, handed to a callee that keeps it) it is rejected once the caller owned the temporary — it was left owned by nothing and leaked (h378–h381) | §1 | no: the calculus names every argument (a temporary is a binding its statement makes, and its view borrows it) | extension: safe code is rejected more; the compiler brought into line with A1's named arguments |
| A closure's env is in the panic journal: each function's closure bindings as one frame-table entry (value and owner slot), an argument's closure temporary and a struct literal argument's owned fields while the call runs (h375–h377) | §7.2 | no: A2's panic step drops what each slot owns, a package's environment with it | none to the proof: the compiler brought into line with A2's panic step |
| An assignment's value is lent to whatever reads it (the arm of a `?` / `??` statement it ends drops nothing); where a join that may select the target lends, the value is copied in (h371–h374) | §7.6 | no: in the calculus a conditional is a statement (`ite`) whose arms are assignments, and `= x x` moves the value over itself | none to the proof: the compiler brought into line with the calculus's reading of a join assigned to a binding |
| A binding in a borrowed state is refused as an `inout` argument where the callee may replace that parameter (it used to be released under the binding's owner); a `: ~` copy of a `% Drop` struct from a local owner takes the owner's drop flag, so a replacing call makes the copy the owner and the source moved; a `^` checks its expression's calls first (h367–h370, h295 folded in) | §2.4, §7.6 | no: the calculus has no borrowed binding as an `inout` argument (`paramOK_inout`) | none to the proof: the compiler brought into line with `paramOK_inout`, which never admitted the shape |
| `arc_get` lends every `Arc`'s payload, a trait object's and a cycle-capable one's too (it used to copy those), and the frozen view it lends is tracked for every `Arc`; a method called through a trait object is summarised as the union of its impls (what it changes and what it replaces, an `inout` receiver changing the box) before the summaries resolve (h364–h366) | §6.5, §7.7 | no: a call through an interface (`callc`) takes its parameter modes from the interface, which `Implements` holds every impl to; `Arc` is A3 | none to the proof for the `dyn` call: the compiler's summary had taken less than the impls do, and now takes their union, as `Implements` does. An `( Arc %Trait )` read is accepted where it was a compile error: the same frozen lend A3 must cover for every `arc_get` |
| An owned binding with a drop flag is journaled with the flag, which the drain reads (it was read when the binding was recorded): a value moved into a literal, a `sink` parameter, a closure's capture or a `dyn` box is not released again by a later panic (h361–h363) | §7.2 | no: A2's panic step drops what each slot owns when the panic runs, and a moved owner's slot (`⟨own, none⟩`) owns nothing | none to the proof: the compiler brought into line with A2's panic step |
| A crossing to another thread (`thread_spawn`, `spawn*`, `chan_send`, a pool's return, a `Locked`'s value going in and out) checks at run time that it leaves no owner of a handle whose copies are one state behind, and panics otherwise; a trait object's box is checked through its vtable (h349–h355, h360) | §6.2, §6.5 | no: threads are A3 | closes the open hole recorded below (a library handle's copies on two threads) with a run-time check rather than a rule: A3 must take the crossing's precondition, every owner of such a block goes along, from the check, as a panic where it fails |
| A `% Sync` marker on a `Locked`, a `Channel` or a `Pool` no longer vouches for its value, which faces Send where the container is reached from several threads; an `RwLocked`'s value faces Sync (h357, h358) | §6.5 | no: threads are A3 | extension: safe code is rejected more |
| A binding read out of a library handle field, consumed or taken by a thread, takes its struct along: the struct is dead until given a fresh value (h348, h356) | §7.6 | no: library handles are the trusted base (B) | extension: safe code is rejected more |
| An `inout` parameter given a binding whose value another owner keeps (a field read, a closure's capture) is given a copy; a closure's `inout` parameter is never an owner of its own (h345–h347) | §7.6 | no: the calculus has no borrowed binding as an `inout` argument (`paramOK_inout`) | none to the proof: the compiler gives such a parameter an owned copy, which the calculus types as a fresh owner passed `inout` |
| The journal's elision knows the runtime's panicking entries (`nurl_panic_index`, a CPU tier's `_missing` gate, the crossing check), so a function whose only panic is one of them keeps its registrations (h359) | §7.2 | no | none to the proof: which calls may panic is the implementation's to know |
| A trait object `%Trait` is Send only when its trait declares `: Send`, Sync only with `: Send Sync`; a `: Send` / `: Sync` supertrait is answered by the derivation at each impl (it used to demand an impl block of the marker, which never exists) (h344) | §6.5 | no: threads are A3 | extension for trait objects: safe code that moved or shared one is rejected more; a `: Send` / `: Sync` supertrait is accepted where the derivation holds, which only lets the object cross. A1 and A2 have no threads, so no lemma moves; A3 must take a trait object's thread facts from its trait's promise, since the object's type names no field |
| A positive `% Send` / `% Sync` outside the standard library is `unsafe % Send T { }`; `% NotSend`, `% NotSync`, `% Frozen` and `% Resource` stay safe (they only add checks); the `in` / `sync` / `nocapture` claims and the standard library's own markers are in the trusted base, and `--unsafe-report` lists the program's | §3, §6.4, §6.5 | no: threads are A3 and the trusted base is B, neither started | extension: safe code is rejected more. A1 and A2 have no markers and no threads, so no lemma moves; A3 takes an `unsafe % Send` / `unsafe % Sync` as a hypothesis of the base, as B takes an `unsafe` body |
| `dist/heartbeat.nu`'s thread holds the node's liveness (an `Atomic` and a copy), not the table; `PkMemberTable` is `% NotSend`; `mqtt_listen` takes its client (`sink`); one open hole listed: a library handle's copies on two threads | §6.2, §6.5 | no: threads are A3 | standard library only (no rule changes) and a stated exception to the guarantee; A3 must model library handles' sharing copies (a copy is a second owner of one state), where the open hole sits |
| A destructor that panics while a panic's journal drains is caught by the same `recover`; the first message is kept; the interrupted drain is ended by the frame that catches it | §7.2 | no: A2's drops run no program code (`DropSlot`) | none to the proof: runtime behaviour outside the calculus, stated in `CLAIM.md` (destructors as code are a planned refinement) |
| Formal model: `inout` of every owning type (a struct, an enum, a closure, not only a `Vec`), a field replaced in place (an `inout` struct taken apart and assigned again), summary kinds on such a parameter inside it | §2.4, §2.12 | yes: `paramOK`, `FnOK`, `StmtT.unpair` (rules of A2, done) | extension of the calculus to programs it did not express before: `paramOK` and `FnOK` accept more parameter types, `unpair` more modes (`canDo .replace` for `own`), `FnOK` asks `kindFits` of summaries. Proof-breaking for the lemmas that took an `inout` parameter to be a `Vec` (`AbsOK`, `allowed_canDo`, `callPostAux_lift`, `rr_sub`, `rhs_move`, `CtxWF.unpair`, the `unpair` cases of preservation, progress and renaming): each re-proven, `check.sh` passes |
| `Fiber` is a sealed (`raw`), move-only handle with its own drop: `fiber_join` takes it (`sink`), a joinable handle dropped unjoined detaches its fiber (`nurl_fiber_detach`), and a handle from `spawn` / `fiber_current` is not joinable (h610–h615) | §2.1, §3.3d | no: threads are A3 and the trusted base is B | standard library only (no rule changes): the existing move and sealing rules now apply to the fiber handle; A3 must model a joinable fiber's block as owned by its one handle |
| A select waiter is a counted (`Shared`) block: a channel it is armed on holds a share until the disarm, and the `?? { }` lowering polls once before it makes a waiter (h616) | §3.3d | no: threads are A3 and the trusted base is B | standard library and lowering only (no rule changes): the select machinery called from safe code no longer leaves a raw address behind |
| A handle field of a sealed representation (`. key d`, at any depth of a field chain, or a binding read out of one, or a copy of that binding) handed to a callee that may change it in place — a container mutator's first argument, a summary that changes, reallocates or takes the argument, or a function of the program compiled later, decided by its final summary at module end — is a write to that field, refused outside `unsafe` (h459, h467, h617–h620, h638) | §3.3d | no: the trusted base is B, not started | extension: safe code is rejected more. A1 and A2 have no sealed representations, so no lemma moves; B must state a sealed struct's invariant as kept by every call its fields reach |

| A join handed to a `sink` is copied on the paths where an arm lent; the fresh arm moves; a value that cannot be copied is refused (h934–h936) | §1, §7.6 | no: a `sink` argument is a live owner the call moves (`CallT` (`sink`)), and a borrow is never moved | none to the proof: a copy is a clone call; the refusal is an extension |
| Elements changing through any copy of a handle (a clear, a set, a push) end the borrows of what was read out of the elements. A copy of a binding's own value (a field of it, or all of it handed back by name or by a call that returns it whole) and a part of the value the change went through stay readable (agora #118, #267) | §2.10, §2.11 | no: a handle is a cell (`Val.href c`); a `.self` dependency asks only for its root's node, with any elements (`HrefAt`), so element drops keep it unless the elements hold views (`Eff.kills .drop .self = hv`; nurlc's `vdd_` guard). An element read is `.inner` (`elemE`), which they end | #267 rejects more; #118 accepts more, inside what the calculus already allows (`HrefAt .self`), so no lemma reopens. A change through a shared copy of a handle, rather than an owner or an `inout`, is `StmtT.editB` (agora #295): it ends every borrow of the elements of each root of the copy's path and keeps, on a push, the copies of a same-typed root's whole handle. The further keeps #118 accepts beside it (a copy of the binding's own value, a part of the value the change went through) are outside the calculus (`CLAIM.md`) |

The rules are stricter in two places: a holder gains no view beyond what its `letd` records up front, views of older bindings (nurlc: any view of an older binding, at any time; Agora #782), and it is no `inout` argument (nurlc checks the callee at its end only, which public #1030 shows open) |

| A holder whose drop may read views, moved on some paths only, is still dropped on the others: the drop check asks a maybe-moved holder as one that lives, so a source of its views that ends after the join is refused (agora #252) | §2.14 | yes: the #224 row's check, made to reject more | an extension (safe code is rejected more); formal's DtorSafe join rule (`EntryWeaker` keeps a holder that may run a destructor, with its `held`, live or dead), done in A2-drop.2 (#241) |

| A view a callee stores into an `inout` argument — a whole assignment in its last statement (the literal's raw parameters are kept, as the next statement would have decided), or a field of an `inout` struct of views, at any argument position — are dependencies of that argument where the call runs (agora #259). | A store that replaces a field a local reader still views hands the reader the old value instead of dropping it. Identity is decided at run time: the struct's first field that has one, else a library handle's control block. The reader then owns the value, and its consumers (literal, push, `sink`, return) move it. They zero the field only while it still holds that value (h196, agora #265) | §2.12 | no: the replace is §2.12's take-apart-and-rebuild, with the old field moved into the reader's owner cell (DynCell, #185): own on the hand-over path, a borrow of the field elsewhere | none to the proof: per-path ownership, as DynCell encodes it |
| A struct's binding class asks precisely whether its fields can hold a view: a library handle (Arc, Box, Rc, Shared, a map, a channel, a lock and its guards) holds one exactly when its type arguments can, a trait object or a closure always, a struct by its fields (agora #269; probes arc_field_returned_in_an_option, arc_field_of_a_sink_param_returned_in_an_option, box_field_returned_in_an_option; guard arc_of_views_field_returned_in_an_option) | §2.12 | no: `Ty.holdsView` is structural (`vec t` holds a view exactly when `t` does, `fn` always; a Box is a one-element `vec`, an Arc A3.1's `arc t`), so the class is the calculus's own answer | none to the proof: it accepts more, but only what the calculus already types; a lent handle stored into the struct, by a literal or by a field assignment, is copied (`cloneif`), so the struct owns it |
| A binding read out of a field — a field viewer — that takes the field over (consumed, or handed to a parameter that takes it) ends every borrow of a path overlapping that field: another viewer of it, a viewer of a field of it or of one holding it (agora #271). A binding bound from a field viewer by name (`: R b a`) views the same field, with that field's path, so it may be handed over as the viewer itself may and takes the field (and, for a library handle field, the struct, h348) with it | §7.6 | yes for the first part: two viewers of one field were each a borrow of it, and consuming one left the other a copy of a freed handle (a heap-use-after-free); the second part only moves a binding from `borrow` to `fborrow`, which the first part covers | the first part extends (rejects more); the second relaxes (accepts handing the re-bound viewer to a parameter that takes it) with the same take-over codegen already emits for the viewer it was bound from (#268) |
| A value block's bare-binding tail is read where the block ends, from that block's own path: a block — bound by `:`, a `?` / `??` arm, or another block's tail — whose tail is an owner its path moved or may have moved, or a view already invalidated, is refused (agora #274; probes join_block_arm_selects_a_consumed_owner, match_block_arm_selects_a_consumed_owner, value_block_tail_is_a_consumed_owner) | §2.1 | no: the block's value is its tail, `var x` | none to the proof: an extension (rejects more); reading a moved or invalidated tail is the `var` rule's live-entry premise, as in #242's arm reads |
| A field a `sink` takes (`( take . h buf )`, a field path of a binding) is moved until it is given a value again. These are refused, definitely or after a `?` / `??` join: a call reading that path, a field borrow of it, a call in a `^`'s expression reading it, the path itself handed back (`^ . h buf`) or read into a literal's field (`@ Holder { . h buf }`, a `pendread` row), binding it, handing the binding on whole (a consume, a store, a pending call or keep, `^ h`), and an exit (a `^`, the body's end) while that binding is an `inout` parameter. A store to the field, a new value for the binding, or a new declaration of its name makes it usable again. A take in a loop's body stays at the head of the next round and after the loop when the binding was bound before the loop (__bck_fmv_carry, agora #351) (agora #280; probes vec_field_taken_by_a_sink_then_pushed, string_field_taken_by_a_sink_then_pushed, struct_with_a_taken_field_handed_on_whole, struct_with_a_taken_field_returned_whole, inout_field_taken_by_a_sink_then_used_by_the_caller; compiler/tests/diag_moved_field, diag_moved_field_inout; agora #351: field_taken_by_a_sink_in_a_loop_then_taken_again, field_taken_by_a_sink_in_a_loop_left_by_break_then_pushed, taken_field_returned_itself, taken_field_returned_in_a_literal, struct_given_a_literal_of_its_taken_field). The other routes, a path over or under the taken one, the holder handed on whole by a store, a literal, a value block or a join's arm (`? c h h2`, `? c { h } h2`), a take through a binding that reads the field, a closure, a panic and a `% Drop` that reads the field, are closed in t929*.md (AGORA-929), and a panic in the call that takes the field or in the one that refills it in t939.md (AGORA-939) | §2.1, §2.12 | no: A2 has no partial move. A field a `sink` takes is the struct taken apart (`StmtT.unpair`) and the field handed to the call. After that neither is live, so a read of the field or a whole use of the struct fails the `var` rule's live-entry premise. A store to the field is the struct assigned again (`StmtT.assign`), and an `inout` parameter is live at the body's end (`FnOK`) | none to the proof: an extension (rejects more). A `% Drop` struct's destructor would run on the empty field, where A2's `unpair` runs none: a sink may not take a field the destructor reads (t929-unwind.md) |

| A view stored into a container through a borrow of it (a copy of its owner's handle through an identity call, a field viewer `. o kids`, an element `( vec_at vs 0 )`, a field viewer of an element) makes the container's OWNER depend on the view's source, not only the borrow: the owners are found by following the borrow's sources through any further borrows to the bindings that are none (`__bck_store_owners`, agora #292) | §7.6 | yes: made to reject more (a read of the owner after the view's source ended was accepted, a heap-use-after-free) | an extension (rejects more), of the form A2 types as `StmtT.editBV` (agora #295): a push or set through a borrow of elements that hold views makes every view-holding root of the borrow's path (`memDeps`, the owners and `inout` parameters it leads to) hold the stored value's dependencies (`holdThru`), and ends what `editB` ends, with a root's whole-handle borrows too when its type holds views (`killThruV`). That is what nurlc now does at `__bck_store_owners`. A store through a borrow inside closure code, or rooted at a plain borrowed parameter, is not typed yet and stays outside the claim (CLAIM.md) |
| A value given an owner and a view of that owner at once (`: S o @ S { sv b }`, sv a slice of b; either field order; a String and a view of its bytes) is refused where it is built: the store of b into o meets o's dependency on b made on the same row, the literal that takes both (`bck_diag_self_view`). Checked across rows the record is no path state, and refused an early-return guard and sibling arms; the two-step, moved-out, temporary and field-of-b forms are agora #563. A closure's capture, stored with it for the checker only, is no field. nurlc let such a value view its own field (`__bck_self_view_note`), and growing or replacing that field left the view dangling inside it (agora #294). A view of another binding's buffer beside an owner of its own keeps compiling | §2.10 | yes: made to reject more (a value that views its own field) | extends: rejects more; A2's SelfFree: an owned value views none of its own buffers (`ViewsOK.dropSelf`, `HeapOK.self`) |
| formal A2 (Agora #295, design c): a destructor's code may change elements through a borrow when the type it takes holds no views. `DtorOK` used to ask `¬ ThruFn P f`; it now asks `ThruFn P f → t.holdsView = false`, which is `CallT`'s `ThruFn` premise for the call the frame's end makes (`Step.scopeDEnd`): a `letd` of a type that holds no views is unarmed (`dbind = false`, `CtxWF.view`), and an unarmed entry, revived there, holds nothing, whatever its `letd` recorded through `HeldUp` (`Entry.revive`). No new invariant. The glue definitions (`GlueOK`, `ImplGlueOK`, `EnumGlueOK`, `ClosureGlueOK`) keep `¬ ThruFn P f`. nurlc accepts such a destructor (a struct of two `i` fields whose `% Drop` pushes through `( vec_at loc 0 )` of its own local runs clean); `A2/ThruDtorWitness.lean` types it, safe and leak-free. Still outside: a through-changing destructor of a type that holds views, and through glue | — (no rule of MEMORY.md changes) | yes: `DtorOK` | extends, re-proven in the same change: the scope-end drop in `preserve` derives `callR = []` from the new premise; witnesses that build `DtorOK` pass their `¬ ThruFn` as `fun h => absurd h …`; `ThruClosureWitness`'s body lemmas take any program; no crown statement changes; witness `A2/ThruDtorWitness.lean` |
| formal A2 (Agora #295, design c, glue): drop glue may change elements through a borrow, directly or through the struct's own `% Drop` it runs, when the type it takes apart holds no views. `GlueOK`, `ImplGlueOK`, `EnumGlueOK` and `ClosureGlueOK` used to ask `¬ ThruFn P f`; they now ask `ThruFn P f → t.holdsView = false`, as `DtorOK` does. The glue drop at a scope's end (`glueCall_typed`, through `sinkOf`) derives `CallT`'s `ThruFn` premise as the destructor drop does: the entry of a type that holds no views is unarmed (`dbind = false`, `CtxWF.view`), so revived it holds nothing, whatever its `letd` recorded through `HeldUp` (`Entry.revive`). The glue's own call of the impl, on the `letd` it rebinds the value under, passes none either. No new invariant. nurlc accepts such a struct (a struct holding a `% Drop` field and an `i`, whose own `% Drop` pushes through `( vec_at loc 0 )` of its own local, runs clean); `A2/ThruGlueWitness.lean` types it, safe and leak-free. Still outside: through-changing drop code of a type that holds views | — (no rule of MEMORY.md changes) | yes: `GlueOK`, `ImplGlueOK`, `EnumGlueOK`, `ClosureGlueOK` | extends, re-proven in the same change: `glueCall_typed` takes the `ThruFn` premise, which the glue case of `preserve` derives from the new conditional; witnesses that build glue pass their `¬ ThruFn` as `fun h => absurd h …` (11 sites); no crown statement changes; witness `A2/ThruGlueWitness.lean` |
| A view a callee leaves one argument holding of another argument's own storage — a String parameter's buffer (`string_data src`), stored whole, into a field, pushed into a Vec of views, or through a callee it hands both on to — is a dependency of the caller's argument at the holder's index on its argument at the source's index, decided by the callee's walk where it returns (g_fn_vkeeps, holder/source pairs as A2's outHolds: agora #337), and a temporary at the source's index is refused (agora #301, extending #259's row). An owner passed `inout` is a source like any other, and a holder passed as a field path is its root; a self-call into a local holder whose sources are all parameters is taken as kept, so the holder handed on carries the dependency into the function's own answer (agora #337). Safe programs it refuses: a cycle of two or more functions answers "may" (agora #371). Not yet tracked: a view parameter (#259's row) kept in a holder other than the first argument is tied to the first (agora #370). Such a call inside a closure body, leaving a capture or the closure's `inout` parameter viewing another's storage, is refused (agora #312) | §2.10, §2.11 | `inout`: no, A2 already makes it a dependency (`CallT`'s `outHeld`; a view of a parameter's own storage is a view of what the caller passed, its argument root's `.inner`); closures: refused, as A2 refuses it (`CapsOK`, `Implements`; agora #312) | an extension for `inout`; for closures, rejects more |
| A binding's end — where its scope or match arm closes — ends the VIEW borrowers and dependents of its fields too (a view of `. h name` is recorded on the field's path, which `h`'s storage holds); a field's reader of an owner type is left to codegen, which takes or copies it. Storage the caller holds — a parameter, not a local view of one — given whole a view of a binding this function releases is refused, as storage that depends on one already was: where a scope ends and, since agora #336, at every `^` (bck_param_view_check), both asked of the state after the defers ran. A cursor payload's field view ends with its arm, as the payload's own does (the transfer #336 made to the matched binding is withdrawn, agora #409: nurlc did not end it on a change of that binding's payload made in place through a second match); a field of it moved out — directly or through a binding that reads it — takes that binding apart (MOVED), as A2's `take` of a path moves its root's value (agora #309, #336). Safe programs it refuses: a holder given a view of an inner block's local, used there and reassigned before the return (A2 types it: `assign` revives the parameter) | §2.10 | yes: made to reject more (field paths were left out of a root's end; an `inout` struct of views given a local's view whole was not asked, then not at a `^` or after a defer) | an extension (rejects more): nurlc brought into line with A2, where a field's storage is part of its root's (a path's `.inner`) and FnOK keeps every `inout` parameter, at the end, holding only what outHolds names, which `lendable` keeps to parameters |
| At a `?` / `??` join, a binding's borrow facts are the union of the arms': its sources (`bs_`), the buffers it views (`vi_`). The field-viewer marks (`fb_`/`fp_`) are kept only where the arms agree (or one arm is dead): a reader that took the field over on one path owns it there, and a viewer's mark past the join would hide a move of it (claude-formal's review of agora #318). Each arm is walked from the facts the join started from, so an arm that re-points a binding no longer hides the other arm's source from a release past the join (agora #311; probes binding_repointed_on_*, option_repointed_on_*) | §2.10 | yes: made to reject more (a binding's sources were whichever arm the walk visited last) | an extension: the join takes the union of the arms' dependencies, as A2's `ite` / `sub` (EntryWeaker) require |
| Inside a closure body, a value that may hold a view — of a binding, a parameter, a capture or a local; anything but a static literal — is refused where it is stored into one of the closure's captures (a whole assignment, a field store, a push or keep the callee really stores, a call that leaves it viewing another's storage) or into the closure's own `inout` parameter. A capture or a parameter of the closure stored whole stands for what it points into, whatever its class: a view, an owner of views, a `% Drop` holder, a closure (agora #331). A keep that only copies stores nothing (agora #312, #272's option (b); probes closure_*, passed_closure_*, drop_holder_closure_*). Not yet: such a value bound to a local of the body first, and a push into a plain Vec parameter of the closure (agora #332) | §2.11 | yes: §2.11's rule, made to reject more: nurlc refuses what A2 refuses for a closure that gives a capture or a parameter a view — `CapsOK` (a capture by name holds no views) and `Implements` (no code outHolds what its interface does not name); a closure that changes a view-holding capture without giving it a view (a pop, a clear, a static literal) stays accepted, as before | an extension: it rejects more; #259's `@vp=` and #301's `@vb=` closure dependencies, which it replaces, have no writers now. #312's first walk let a whole store of a class-3 or class-4 capture through, which `@vp=` had refused; agora #331 refuses it again |
| A `?` / `??` join as a call's argument hands the callee what one of its arms selects. The bindings its identifier arms name get the rows a call made with each of them would (`pendjoin`), so a callee that drops, replaces, grows or moves what it is handed ends their borrows and views at the call, as for the join bound to a name first (agora #314; probes container_join_*, container_match_*; field-path arms are agora #360) | §2.10 | yes: made to reject more (a join argument recorded no row) | none to the proof: A2's ANF binds the join first, and a change through the bound name ends what borrows from every root of its path |
| A handle a function reads out of its parameter (an element `( vec_at p 0 )`, a field, a copy of one, or that call as an argument) is that parameter's storage to its summaries. At the call, the caller's views into the argument's elements' buffers end, but its element handles and its views of the argument's own buffer do not, as through a borrowed element in one function. A handle read past an Arc's payload or a lock's guard is no parameter's storage: the payload is frozen, and the lock is exclusive at run time (agora #315; probes callee_grows_*, callee_forwards_*; the drop half is agora #333) | §2.10 | yes: made to reject more (a buffer move through such a handle was attributed to no parameter) | none to the proof as nurlc's summaries go; outside A2's `in` parameters (`.rootb`, which change nothing), which #295 models: a change through a local derived from a parameter counts against that parameter (#295 map item M5) |
| At a loop head, a reader that a field replace handed the old value on the path back into the head (there it owns that value and borrows nothing) stays the field's viewer it was on the path in, so the next round's replace hands over to it again. | The question "may dropping this value run a `% Drop` that reads a view?" walks every layer of the value's type — struct fields, enum payloads, Vec and Box elements, option payloads, a trait object's impls — with a visited set that ends the search at a type already being asked about, instead of a depth cap that answered "no" nine layers down (agora #340) | §7.6 | yes: made to reject more (a holder whose view-reading destructor sat nine or more layers down was not asked about) | none to the proof: nurlc brought into line with A2, where a value's drop is a use of every view any part of it holds, at any depth |

Its `assign` gives an owner only an owned value, and DynCell's encoding of a per-call result (A2/DYN_DESIGN.md) makes the result depend on its argument, which `assign` refuses for that binding itself (`x ∉ roots E.vdeps`). | A copy of a value that holds views holds the same views (agora #362, #368, #377, #347). What a function makes of what its argument holds is derived from its walk, as #422 derives what it makes of the argument's storage: a parameter that holds views (an owner of them, a Slice, a struct of views) starts its walk holding a pseudo source of its own, `%p`, standing for the caller's views. A value handed back holding them answers `%k` (g_fn_rviews), and another parameter left holding them answers `h>%k` (g_fn_vkeeps; `%K.H` rows at the call). The caller asks what a copy of argument k holds, its views and not its storage (`g_sxe_held`): an owner's dependencies, or what a borrow's sources' elements view. `mem_dup` answers `:%0`, and publishes its operand. A callee with no walk answer (trusted or library code, compiled) whose result can hold views holds what its non-closure arguments hold, and so does a match payload of such a call. A copy taken by the call it is an argument of still holds what it held. A copy of a part of an argument (an element or a field handle, a slice of it; `retpart`) holds what the part holds, not the storage the part is in, and a holder that borrows its value depends on what that value holds, its borrow tying it to the rest. A value that views its own storage — an element viewing a sibling's String, a field viewing another field, a struct of that kind moved in whole — holds views of itself (`vsv_`, recorded where its dependency on itself is dropped): what a copy of a part of it holds includes it. A view field of an owner copied whole (`mem_dup`'s operand, an owner parameter's `%k`, a `%K.H` row: `g_sxe_hctn`) is copied itself, so the copy points where the field does; a view argument itself holds what the elements of its storage view (where it points is its lends'), and a callee that copies the elements of a view it is given — `g_fn_vcopies`, a parameter that is a view of a container (`g_fn_eparams`) — holds those. A bare view handed back holding it answers `%k`. A parameter the body may give a new value (`inout`, owned) holds `%p` only on the paths that do not: `%p` is a dependency the joins and a loop's dependency snapshot follow. A callee with no walk answer of views (trusted, library or `unsafe` code, compiled) leaves each `inout` argument whose type holds views holding what its non-closure arguments hold (`h>%k`). | What a function makes of its parameters' views reaches its callers. (1) A view parameter (an `s` the caller still owns, a Slice, a struct of views; not `inout`, not `sink`) stands for itself as a source in the function's walk (`pv_`, `bck_sx_name`): it has no borrow sources there, the caller's, so a holder or a result given it, or a local copy of it, used to depend on nothing (agora #341, #346, #370, #380, #454: a store through a loop element, a join or an element binding of the function's own parameter lands in that parameter, `hr_`, as a pair of its answer). A part of an argument that owns its storage and holds no view (`vec_at`'s String), read inline as the argument of a call that lends it, is reached through the argument's element, as a binding of it is (`^k`). (3) A view argument consults the callee's `g_fn_vkeeps` pairs as an owner argument does (`bK` rows), so it is tied to the holder the callee stores it in, not to the first argument (agora #370, #346). | A binding that is a borrow on one path and its own value on another (`BorrowedShared ⊔ Owned`: a cursor a loop re-points at a fresh slice, a `?` / `??` arm that gives it a value of its own, a `continue` path) is `MaybeOwned`, no longer a plain borrow. So is a binding given, in one `let` / `=`, a value that may be its own and also borrows (a join with a fresh arm and a view arm, a call that hands back a fresh value on some calls and lends on others), and one a closure that runs gives a new value (`@assign`), and a binding that borrows given a field through itself (`= . cur name …`: codegen stores the value into the binding's copy, which releases it where the binding is given a new value). It stays a borrow to every other rule (what it borrows from may not go first). But given a new value it releases what it owned there, so what was borrowed from it ends. At its scope's end it is released as an owner is, ending what views it, what was stored into it, and the caller's holders given a view of it. A copy of it borrows it as well as what it borrows (`bck_borrow_owners`). Only a view binding the walk has seen given a value that may be its own joins to `MaybeOwned`: one given only views and literals owns nothing on any path. Before, these were read as plain borrows, and re-pointing a borrow frees nothing: open on main for a cursor over a local String's view, and reopened by #422's view parameters for h118 (`cursor_whole_rest_after_replace`) | §2.2, §7.6 | yes: made to reject more (a copy of a binding that may own its value, kept across its re-point or its scope's end, or given to the caller's storage) | none to the proof: A1's state is per path, and on the path where the binding owns its value `StmtT.assign` and the scope's end drop it, ending the borrows of its `.self`. nurlc's join kept only the borrow side of the two paths; `MaybeOwned` keeps both, as `MaybeMoved` does for a move on one path |

Before, the block was read as a fresh value, and o's value was released while the call had handed it back (agora #427) | §7.6 | yes: `= o { ( pick o other k ) }` over an option is refused, as `= o ( pick o other k )` already is | none to the proof: the block's value is its tail call's |

That stop is the designed one for a use of a value moved out: it also stops a later run that only reads what main read after the move without faulting (a scalar field of a struct given away, `. h n`; `string_eq x x` of a String given away); an option or a result capture the body releases moves into the environment as a String does; and the environment releases a capture it took over (one with a Clone) when the body never ran. A growth sweep through that parent, or through another borrow of the same storage (same `bs_`, same `lpa_`), ends the cursor exactly when what grows is a level below the parent (`g_bck_sx_part`, the container's own level), and never when the parent's own buffer moves, which moves only the containers' handles. Before, two lends deep the cursor's source flattened to the outermost owner, and a growth of the container's level counted as "a level further down" (g_bck_end_elem 2), which leaves cursors alone. A closure's capture or `inout` parameter left viewing itself (`h>.rel` or `h>!.rel`) is refused as a view stored into one is (bck_clo_cap_view_diag). | `vec_retain` judges every element before it drops one. The kept elements are swapped to the front, slot for slot, so the `Vec` holds each of its elements exactly once whenever the predicate runs; then the length goes down to the kept ones and the others are dropped through `mem_drop_range` (`nurl_vec_drop_range_dtor`, the record `vec_truncate` and `vec_clear` use). A predicate that panics leaves a `Vec` the drain drops once per element; a destructor that panics leaves a `Vec` of the kept elements, and the others still to go are dropped all the same. Before, each element that went was dropped inside the loop with the `Vec` at its old length, and a panic's drain dropped the `Vec` over slots already given up. | `= o e` where `e` was bound from a value block whose tail is o (`: e { o }`, `: e { { o } }`; `__btail`) is asked by its key, as a cursor over o is: on the runs `e` holds o's own value, o keeps it and nothing is released. Before, o's value was released and stored back freed (agora #667) | §7.6 | no: codegen only (a run-time key compare) | none to the proof: assigning a value to its own owner is the identity |
| A `: ~` binding of a value block's tail binding (`: ~ e { : T s … s }`) is that binding to its drop flag, as the `:` one is (#615): a cursor given a value of its own later hands over only that one, by its per-run own flag (#781), so the owner link may stand (agora #668) | §7.6 | no: §7.6 already says a moved value leaves its owner and a cursor's owner keeps its own | none |
| A `~` loop element whose type has a `% Drop` of its own and cannot be copied, handed to a `sink` parameter, is refused (its slot cannot be emptied: the container would run the destructor on the emptied value); one that can be copied is handed a copy. And `^ b` of a local binding that may hold a lend (`= b x`, x a loop element or a vec_at binding) answers per call whether it lends when its type cannot be copied (mem_hown_mark_dyn), as one that can be copied already did through the copy; before, it answered "owned" and the caller released a value its owner still held (agora #669) | §7.6 | no: §7.6 already forbids handing over what another owner drops and says a lend is answered as a lend; nurlc now applies both to `% Drop` values that cannot be copied | none |
| A `;` defer inside a loop body runs at a way out above it, and the walk reads it there with what the earlier round left (agora #670). The walk carries the bindings such a defer keeps (`__bck_loop_kept`: every `let` in scope at a defer site of the body, above it — what codegen keeps) across the back-edge and the loop's exit with their borrows, does not end them at the body's end, and lets go of what borrowed from an earlier round's value when the name is bound again (`__bck_kept_rebind`: that value is parked to the cleanup); the exit's replay (`bck_exit_maybe_defers`, `bck_defers_release_ret`, `bck_end_post_defer`) reads a defer further down a loop around it. One acceptance: a `^` handing back an arm's own binding while a defer frees an outer binding of that name (the defer never reaches the returned value) | A2 has no `defer`; the calculus's claim covers defer bodies read as code at the function's exits, the bindings they read living to the function's end — this change makes codegen run a loop's defer at every exit the earlier rounds armed it for, and the walk check those reads with the values the rounds left |
| A `~` loop's element that a `;` defer of the loop's body reads is a borrow of the container's owners (its `fe_`) from the loop's end to the function's exit: bck_borrow_begin at the loop-block row (__bck_fe_defer_keep), and an enclosing loop keeps it across its back-edge and exit as it keeps a `:` binding of its body (__bck_loop_kept). The defer runs at the exit and reads the element's slot there; ended with its loop, a change to the container after the loop went unchecked and the defer read freed memory (agora #672). In codegen a defer body sees no slot of an element its loop bound (`__eslot_dd`, __fe_has_slot): the round's registers are no values at the exit, so it reads the element from its own binding, and its walk refuses a hand-over of it or of its field (a borrow; a `pendfx` row) | §2.10, §5.3 | no rule of the model: §2.10's borrow of an element, kept as long as the defer that reads it, as §5.3 keeps a `:` binding a defer reads (agora #491). | The Send/Sync derivation reads what it is asked about, not a name: a generic struct's instance through the fields and enum payloads the instance has (they are registered under the instance's own name, and the walk looked under the generic's bare name and found none — `( Wrap i )` of `: Wrap [T] { ( Rc T ) held }` crossed a thread); every type argument of a generic as the type that was written (an option was a slug of the mangled name the walk could not take apart — `( Vec ?( Rc i ) )` crossed); an `ArcWeak T` as the `Arc T` it upgrades to, to both questions (T Send and Sync, at every depth; and an `ArcWeak` in an `Arc`'s payload is that Arc, not an unsynchronised share handle); an `Rc`'s `Weak` as the `Rc` it upgrades to (neither Send nor Sync). A `% NotSync` on an instance outranks an `unsafe % Sync` on its generic in every rule that asks whether a type synchronises itself (the frozen `Arc` payload's excuse, the by-value change summary, the frozen walk, the `Arc`-backed / `Rc`-backed handle tests), as it already did in the crossing checks. The derivation, and the frozen-`Arc` rule's walk for a value that holds an `Arc`, read a type whole: every type it reaches is asked once, and the set of types asked is what ends the walk on a type that holds itself — a budget of ten levels answered "nothing found" below it (an `Rc` eleven structs down crossed a thread; an `Arc` eleven structs down lent its payload for writing). The holder walk keeps an answer only for a type whose walk was whole: a provisional "holds none" for a type still being read was final to a type that holds it back (agora #675, #683, #690, #693, #711) | §6.5 | no rule of the model. It refuses more (the tree sweep refuses no file of main; the newly refused programs are the PR's own probes and diag tests) and accepts more in three places where main refused by how it read a name: a type argument after an `Arc` / `RwLocked` first argument was held to Sync with that argument's payload (`( Two ( Arc i ) Cell )` moved); an instance's `unsafe % Send` / `unsafe % Sync` marker was found only on the last argument of the generic around it; the library's `ArcWeak` in an `Arc`'s payload was taken for a share handle with one unsynchronised state (`: Node { ?( ArcWeak Node ) parent }` in an `Arc`) | none to the proof: the model has no generics, no marker traits and no name-keyed tables; its threads rule is stated over the types a value holds, which is what nurlc now reads |

| The calls of a returned expression are judged before its `ret` row (agora #680): the rows the walk decides at its end (`ppends`: a field of a borrow handed to a `sink` — `pendfx` —, a keeping call, a call's lend) were recorded behind the `ret` row, which ends the path, and never walked; they are recorded before it now, after an `expr` row that reads what the return read, as a statement's are (`__bck_ret_flush_ppends`; the views-itself case did so already, agora #584). `^ ( keep . c name )`, `^ + 1 ( keep . c name )` and the same call as the implicit tail are refused where they freed a field the owner still held, as the call bound first already was. ( arc_get [App] mine ) ev { Got p → … }` makes `p` a view of `mine`'s payload, so changing it is refused by the frozen-Arc rule as for the option field beside it, and `?? . ( vec_at [App] apps 0 ) ev { Got p → … }` makes it a borrow of `apps`. A field of a fresh call's result stays the arm's own; a payload only read stays accepted | §2.10, §6.5 | no: a payload of what a call lends is `CallT`'s view of the call's source, whichever field the read took it out of | extends: rejects more |
| A view of an Arc's payload stays one through the owners it is kept in (agora #682): a Vec it is pushed into, a map it is put in, a struct a Vec holds, a struct a helper builds over its Arc parameter. What is lent back out of it is one: a `??` payload, a value a call lends out of it whose type can hold a view (`vec_at`, `vec_get`, `map_get`, a helper's element), a field of it, a borrow of it. The frozen-payload rule then refuses a change through it, as for the view bound directly. A struct's view field given one after the struct was built makes the struct one (`ab_`), as its literal does. The owner itself is not one: pushing into it, truncating or clearing it, moving it changes the owner, not the payload | §6.5 | no: the model's frozen `arc t` refuses a change through every view of the payload, wherever the view is kept; nurlc now follows the view through the owners that hold it | extends: rejects more |

| A closure's effects on its captures — what it changes, frees, gives a new value, the captures it holds — are those of every literal its closure node may be, read at each run (`__clo_run_attr`, agora #686): gen_closure_expr records them under the literal (`le#` / `lf#` / `la#` / `lc#`), and a run through an alias, an alias of an alias, an assignment, a `?` / `??` / block value, a literal chosen by an arm, one assigned in an arm, or a function it is handed to (which counts as running it) ends the views of the capture as the direct call does. | A closure binding declared a write-back (`mem_put_back`, a raw primitive) is stored into an element slot as it is, as a binding of every other type is: it was read out of storage and returns to it, in its own slot or another (two elements changing places). | What a container's release drops leaves the container's storage before the first destructor runs. `vec_clear`, `vec_truncate`, `vec_retain` and `deque_clear`, for an element type whose drop runs code of the program's own (`mem_drop_runs_code`), move the elements to go into a buffer of their own (the runtime frame's stack where they fit, the heap past that — Agora #748; the container's count short and its buffer kept), and drop them from there in index order (`mem_move_drop` → `nurl_vec_move_drop_dtor`; the release's record owns a heap buffer and frees it at its end, a panic's drain included). Before, the elements were dropped out of the container's own buffer, its count already short: a push wrote over the next element to go (never dropped), the release dropped the pushed element while the container held it too, and a push that grew the buffer freed the storage the release walked (heap-use-after-free). Other element types keep the in-place release, instruction for instruction. No checker rule changes (agora #709) | §7.2 | no: a library and runtime change (stdlib/core/vec.nu, stdlib/std/deque.nu, stdlib/runtime_core.c) and one raw primitive. A container's release is outside the calculus; the change restores what the model takes for granted of one: each element dropped once, by the one release that holds it | none to the proof |
| The same-Rc rule (a call that may drop, replace or move what an `( Rc T )` reaches ends the borrows through every other handle that may be the same value) asks which Rc values two names may share by the SETS of Rc types they reach, where it compared one type per name by its spelling. What a call may change through an argument is everything the argument's type reaches (`__rc_reach`: fields by the instance's layout, enum payloads, inline members, type arguments, an Rc's payload — an Rc held in the payload of an Rc, and a `Weak` as the `Rc` it upgrades to), joined with what the shares it borrows from reach; a buffer it may MOVE is in its own storage unless the call was handed a value lent out of it. A view's source is a view of an Rc's payload where the source is, or holds in its own storage, that Rc (`__rc_direct`: fields at any depth, a Box's content — not a container's buffer, not an Rc's payload). The two meet or do not. On main a struct holding a share was another type than the share (`%H` vs `%Rc__…`), a Vec of shares or a payload of shares no Rc type at all, and an Rc two structs down none. The rule also asks the callee's irealloc summary (a callee that moves the buffer of an ELEMENT of what it is handed — `vec_reserve` of `( vec_at ( rc_get a ) 0 )` — ends what points into the elements of the other handle's payload), and so does the rule for a change made through a borrow (`( growelem q )`, q a `vec_at` of a Vec of Vecs: a level below the borrow's own buffer). A call handed a borrow of the very source is that borrow's own rows' for it, unless the borrow's own type reaches an Rc the source is or holds (a copy of a handle read out of an element is another handle on it). A library dropper or element replacer handed a value whole (`vec_clear`, `vec_put` of a Vec of shares) drops handles, which a view through another handle — a count of its own — outlives: what it changes of a payload is the value's own storage's, unless dropping an element runs a `% Drop` of the program's (agora #712, #497, #592, #601 item 2) | §2.10 | yes: made to reject more (a view through one handle read after a call changed the same Rc value through another: a struct holding a share, a Vec of shares, a share a call hands back, a payload holding shares, a share two structs down, an element grown by a helper) | extends: rejects more; in A2/A3 every share of a block reaches the same payload, and what reaches the block — a field, an element, a payload — reaches it through the share it holds: a change through any of them kills what depends on the block through any share. It refuses more at run time (a program's own `Weak` holding a share left behind now panics at the crossing; on main it crossed uncounted and two threads changed one counter) and accepts more in two places where main refused by how it read a name: a program's own `Rc` of numbers captured by a thread, and its own generic `Arc` holding a Cell moved into one | none to the proof: the model has no generics, no marker traits and no name-keyed tables; its threads rule is stated over the types a value holds, which is what nurlc now reads |

Each now has a `pendsink` row (a nameless value noted under a name no program spells, `%rcvN`), swept as a call handed another handle: what the callee's final summaries drop, replace or move of an Rc's payload through it ends the views another handle took. A `( rc_new x )` argument whose payload type reaches no Rc is exempt (no other handle). The function's own drop summary records the same: a sink argument (unless a library dropper is handed it whole and no `% Drop` of the program's runs), and each parameter a nameless argument names whose Rc types meet the argument's (`( ch2 ( rc_clone h ) )`). A binding whose value was read through a borrow not known in full is so (`pf_`), a holder a view was stored into reads what the view is read through, a `~` loop's element and cursor what the loop's container is, and a closure call's result any payload. Where the view's source holds that Rc only in its elements' storage (a Vec of shares; `__rc_direct` does not meet), the call ends the views so marked, the views through an element not known in full, the holders of views through an element, and the loop elements so marked — not the source's own buffer, its element handles, nor its elements' own fields (agora #733, #726) | §2.10 | yes: made to reject more (a share handed to a `sink` helper that replaces or grows its payload, or forwarded to one; a struct or a Vec of shares handed so; a clone handed to a replacer, at the call or inside a helper; a fresh Rc whose payload holds a share; a payload viewed through a Vec of shares — directly, through a binding, a `??` payload or a callee's lend — past a change through another handle) | extends: rejects more; in A2/A3 a change through any share of a block kills what depends on the block through any other share, whether the share was handed over (`sink`), is a temporary, or the dependency reaches the block through an element of a container. `rcp_` is nurlc's over-approximation of "depends on the block's contents": a dependency that reaches a container's elements without crossing into a share's block (its own buffer, an element's own field) is not killed, as in the model |

| Early exits in the calculus: `break`, `continue` and a `^` before the end of a function's body are statements of A2 (`Stmt.block`, `Stmt.exit n`, `Stmt.loop` for `while T`). An `exit n` leaves `n + 1` blocks in one step (`ExitAt`, `Step.blockExit`), ending every scope it leaves as that scope's own end does, its drops and destructors run; typing carries the exits a statement may take (`StmtT`'s `Exits` index: `Exits.under`, `Exits.underD`, `Exits.blk`), and a block's end says no more than the context each exit leaves from. Before, the calculus had none of them: a loop was left only from its head and a function returned at its body's end, so nurlc's rules for these exits (`bck_loop`'s join of the `break`s' states, the `while T` exit of agora #453) were not rules of the calculus (agora #871) | §1, §2.6, §2.8, §7.6 | yes: extended, no statement weakened. `StmtT` gains the exit index (`.none` at a function's body and in every crown theorem, which keep their statements); preservation, progress (a statement about to exit is one more final form, `ExitAt`, never a top-level one: `exitAt_none`), the leak theorems, the renaming lemma (`RSetup.liftX`) and A3's thread lemmas cover the new forms; `ExitWitness` types a `break` out of `while T` that drops a live owned `Vec`, a `continue`, and an early `^` across a scope with its caller, each run safe and leak-free | a gain: the proof's claim now covers programs that leave a loop or a function early. The frames that are not scopes (`recover`, a borrow's, a freeze's, a lock's, an element's) take no exits (`Exits.none`); in source each holds a call, so no exit crosses one (`README.md`) |
| A `break` or `continue` in a closure literal written inside a `~` loop, the closure body having no loop of its own, is refused with a diagnostic naming the reason (`gen_loop_jump`: a loop records the closure depth it was opened at, `g_loop_clo_depth`, beside its labels; a jump at another closure depth sits in a closure body written inside the loop, whose labels are the enclosing function's blocks). Before, the jump branched to the enclosing function's loop label from the closure's own function and clang refused the module (`use of undefined value '%loop_exit_3'`; agora #871; diag_break_in_closure_body, diag_continue_in_closure_body, diag_break_after_loop_in_closure_body, closure_loop_jumps) | §5.4.1, §2.11 | no: in the calculus a closure body is a callee's body, its exits its own body block's, and an exit never crosses a call frame (`Exits.none` under `rbind` / `frozen` / `catchR`); nurlc brought into line | none to the proof: it refuses only programs that never compiled (clang failed on them); a loop written inside the closure body keeps its own `break` / `continue` |
| A binding a closure captured by pointer, given what a call or a join may lend of what dies with the closure (a local or a parameter of it, a capture its env took over; directly, through a generic, a pick, a join, a value block's tail, in a loop, under `recover`), is given a copy where it lent, by the call's answer at run time, and owns it, as an `inout` parameter is (agora #438) and as #864 did for the closure's own parameter by name. Before, the capture was left a borrow of the closure's local, released when the closure returned: a heap-use-after-free after the call. A lend of the capture itself or of another capture is left as it was (it aliases, as the same line outside a closure does; the call site's missing tie to the other capture is AGORA-882) (AGORA-872; probes closure_local_lent_to_a_captured_option_owner_runs_clean and the other capture_lent_* / *_capture_lent_* probes, guards cursor_capture_given_back_itself_by_a_closure_runs_clean, capture_given_a_fresh_value_by_a_closure_runs_clean). A type with no copy is refused there instead, at module end where the callee's lends are known only then (probes capture_lent_*_of_a_type_with_no_copy*). The ownership engine and tools/ncore read the copied store as owned, so they no longer refuse the copyable shapes | §7.4 | yes: made to reject more (a lend of a type with no copy into a capture); otherwise codegen copies where it stored a lend | none to the proof: A2 hands a changed capture to the opened body as an `inout` argument (CAPTURES_DESIGN.md) and `StmtT.assign` into an `inout .replace` place types only an owned value; the copy is that owned value |

Before, the walk ended o's borrows at the `=` and took the call's lend of `o` itself, a lend of nothing, so `= o ( id other )  = o ( id o )  = other …` then a read of `o` compiled and read freed memory. | The ownership engine hands a replaced owner's old value only to a reader that a statement before the replacement linked to it: a `:` binding of a value naming it (by name, a call's lend, a payload) or an `=` of it by name. A pop of a number is that element read first (`vec_at b ( dec ( len b ) )`, a number is a copy) and then the truncation. A remove of a number is each later element set one place down (`vec_set`), then the truncation. Before, the grow was lost at the slice copy, and a view of the element beside the call, or read after it, read freed memory (AGORA-933; probes sink_vec_of_slices_grown_beside_a_view_of_its_element, vec_of_slices_grown_by_a_callee_ends_the_callers_view_of_its_element) | §2.10 | yes: the call-alias and view-ending rules | extension: rejects more, accepts nothing new; A2 already refuses the call (`CallT`'s `ThruFn P f → callR Γ args = []`: the grow is an `edit true` through a borrow that is not fresh, landing in memory an argument views), so nurlc now agrees with the calculus |
| A slice parameter a literal copies into a field it owns by type, of elements that hold views (`[s`), is a buffer of its own holding copies of them. To the borrow walk it is `__nurl_slice_copy(…)` (g_fn_rviews `:^0`): what the elements view, not the slice the parameter views (a by-value slice parameter is a view of the caller's, `pv_`). To the engine and tools/ncore it is `__nurl_slice_copy_v` (`in` → `own`, holding what the argument's elements hold, `- 0 * held`). A caller's `^k` (or `^k %k`) answer views nothing of a temporary whose elements own no storage (a scalar or a view, `__rv_views_arg` / `__rv_elem_owns_nothing`): the temporary owns only its buffer of them, so it is released after the call, and the walk ties the result to what its elements point into. Before, on main 08e56f54: `( wraps ( mks ) )` with `@ wraps [s t → SBag { ^ @ SBag { t 2 } }`, the same of a lending call's temporary and of a binding, leaked the temporary's buffer; the guards (the copy read after the String its elements view was given a new value, bound and through a lending call's temporary) stay refused (agora #935) | §7.6 | no: what was a leak is released; a copy whose elements view a String still depends on it | witness, no rule changed: `A2/SliceCopyViewWitness.lean`. The copy is typed as A2 types a copy, a call with an owned result and no lends (`CallT` gives `callRes`, `copy_typed`): it holds every kind of dependency on what its argument held and nothing rooted at the argument (`copy_holds_a`, `copy_free_of_temp`), so A2 and nurlc's new answer agree. A value's memory dependencies are its `vdeps`, and `kill` ends a binding by an effect on a root among them. The typed copy survives the temporary's release (`copy_survives_temp_release`), where a value depending on the temporary itself, nurlc's old answer, is ended by it (`dep_on_temp_dies`). Replacing what the views point into ends the copy (`copy_dead_after_replace`), and no read of it then types (`read_copy_refused`). nurlc and ncore now answer the copy so |

( mkb ) s`), it answers by that flag as a String does: it is no lend of a parameter (no `__fn_ret_borrow__`, no fresh-slice veto `__fn_ret_slice_mixed__`), and its callers ask the function's module-end constant for it (`rettf##`, `__slice_call_run_bit`, and `@.__nurl_retslc` for a callee compiled after its caller).
