Its report waits for the module's answer. The `unw` row carries the function called (x5: gen_call_body's `call_name`, an instance or an impl, matched to its own call by lexer and start; `` for a closure value). At the row the walk asks the inout exit check of the refilled paths on their own (`g_bck_unw_only`), and their report is parked (`unwrefill`, bck_emit_error). When the module is compiled, and before the IR is written or, under `--check`, discarded, its panic graph is built over the buffered IR with every function live (`__mp_compute`, `dce_unw_resolve`). A parked report stands where the callee may panic or is no function of the module (resolve_unw_refills); a callee reached only through a closure value (`` x5) is asked nothing and counts as one that may panic, so its report stands at once. ncore: `call_may_panic` over every lowered function's calls (`calls_of`, run.py; `may_panic_fns`: `fn` flag bit 3, a `callind`, an unwinding callee with no body, to a fixpoint); `temp_refill` exempts the refill only where its call cannot panic. The call that takes the field, a `sink` argument (`( take . h buf k )`), is the same case: the field is handed over as the call starts, so a panic in it, or in a later call of the same statement, hands the holder back empty. gen_call keeps the calls still running (g_gc_open) and notes each field handed to one (`unwpf`). The `unw` row's x6 lists the fields handed to this call and to calls already done (`__unw_field_args`); a call still running around it has not been handed its arguments yet. Each field a `sink` parameter takes is asked of the exit check as gone, also one taken through a binding that reads it (`: b . h buf`, then `( take b )`: the binding's field path, `__bfpath`, is noted as handed over; AGORA-929 marks it taken only at the move, after the call's `unw` row). This call's own take is parked like a refill; one an earlier call of the statement made is reported at once, as a field taken before any call that may unwind is (`__bck_unw_takes`). ncore: `unwind_check` counts the call's consumed `inout` field paths where the call may panic, a whole binding by the `inout` field its alias reads, except a field a keeping callee is handed a copy of; an earlier call's take is already gone in its lowering. Nothing is asked of a module with no such refill or take. Before, on github#2110 head 9e025429, the refill's call was always exempt: `= . h buf ( make_buf k )` with `make_buf` panicking was accepted and read a null handle after `recover`, and so was `( take . h buf k )` with `take` panicking (agora #939) | §3.3d | yes: a null handle after a panic in the refill's call | none to the proof: the calculus's panic is a way out like any other, and A2 has no partial move; this narrows the exemption github#2110 made to the calls that cannot panic |

A `??` arm reading a field of the matched payload stays open (open/t242.md): the walk's source expression names the matched binding there, not the field, and cannot tell it from a payload taken out whole (`T v → v`), which owns its value. Before, on main 3ad3d7c8, the binding assigned outward read the struct's freed field: heap-use-after-free (AGORA-941, one of AGORA-242's twins) | §7.6 | yes: a borrow of an inner struct's field read after the struct ended, which was a use-after-free | none to the proof: extension, rejects more. ### Audit of h426–h974 (agora #104, 2026-10-30)

The rows from agora #165 to #214 and #205 were written in one pass,
against main `20b200d` and `10b54e3`. In every one of these changes the diff to `docs/MEMORY.md`
only moved the probe ranges. No rule text changed: each change brought
nurlc into line with a rule that already held, or fixed the lowering.
Three findings matter to the proof:

- **No change made a rule accept more than A1 / A2 do.** Only two changes
  accept more: the address-free `??` payload (#206) and the field-name
  capture (#214). Each accepts exactly what the calculus already types
  (`projE … .int = intE`; `CapsOK`).
- **Copies are calls.** "Copied where it lent" (#178–#200, #202) is a call
  of the value's clone, which A2 types as a call with an owned result. A
  clone of a `Vec` of integers can even be written in the calculus
  (`RhsT.at`, `StmtT.push`). The clones' bodies are part of the standard
  library's base (B). A value with no clone is refused, as A2 refuses
  returning or storing the lend.
- **Per-call ownership is inside the calculus, through an encoding.**
  #184, #185, #186, #203, #205 and #206 answer at run time whether a
  result is owned. A2 types none of them directly: a binding is owned or
  borrowed for every run. But such a value is, in the calculus, an owner
  cell plus a borrow. The cell is an `inout` option the caller provides
  and the callee fills when the value is fresh. The borrow reaches the
  cell's payload or the argument, and `lends` names both.
  `A2/DynCell.lean` types this encoding with A2's rules and applies
  `memory_safe` and `no_leak_end` to it, on the standard axioms
  (`pick_ok`, `main_safe`, `main_no_leak`). So these shapes rest on the
  proof for the rules. That nurlc's bit behaves as the cell does (the bit
  set exactly when the cell would be `some`, the drop and the move under
  it) is phase C / D, and until then the probes check it.
  `A2/DYN_DESIGN.md` gives the correspondence. It also says why a direct
  mode `.dyn` would reopen the invariant's root lemmas, which the
  encoding avoids.

Most of the holes in this range were in surface forms the calculus has
only as statements: `?` / `??` joins as values, value blocks and their
tails, `\` tries, `;` defers and fall-off values. Each probe family was
the same shape in another one of these forms.
